{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011CgPyPXqTTjukoHZpME2HB","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"=== BEGIN ARTIFACT ARCHITECTURE.md ===\n# ARCHITECTURE: DrydockStats\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261026 V1 |\n| Description | Module layout, package boundaries, shared types, and technical decisions for the ddkstat LLM log reporter. |\n| Depends On  | None |\n| Provides    | go.mod, cmd/ddkstat, internal/cli, internal/logread, internal/report |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`ddkstat` is a command-line reporter for Drydock's LLM execution log (`logs/llm.jsonl`). It reads a\nJSON Lines file, skips malformed and duplicate lines, narrows the records by optional Target and\ndate filters, and writes one of six CSV reports to standard output. It has no database and no\nnetwork access, and it never writes to the log it reads.\n\n## Technology Stack\n\nDerived from `TECHNOLOGY_STACK.md`, which remains the decision of record.\n\n| Technology | Where it applies |\n|---|---|\n| Go 1.22 or newer, standard library only | All application code; no third-party modules |\n| `go test` and `go vet` | The test runner behind `bin/test.sh` |\n| JSON Lines | The input format, decoded with `encoding/json` |\n| POSIX sh | `bin/test.sh` and the supplied grader `sources/run.sh` |\n\n## Module Layout\n\n```text\ngo.mod                    module declaration, Go 1.22 or newer, no require lines\ncmd/ddkstat/main.go       package main; the only caller of os.Exit\ninternal/cli/             Run, argument parsing, exit codes, usage line\ninternal/logread/         reading, skip rules, de-duplication, record model\ninternal/report/          filters, aggregation, and CSV formatting as separate functions\nbin/test.sh               POSIX launcher for go vet and go test\n```\n\n## Package Boundaries\n\n| Package | Owns | May import |\n|---|---|---|\n| `cmd/ddkstat` | Process start; passes `os.Args[1:]`, `os.Stdout`, `os.Stderr` to `cli.Run`; exits with its result | `internal/cli` |\n| `internal/cli` | Argument parsing, date validation, exit codes, orchestration of read, filter, aggregate, format | `internal/logread`, `internal/report` |\n| `internal/logread` | Opening and reading the log, line numbering, skip and duplicate rules, the `Record` model | standard library |\n| `internal/report` | Filtering, aggregation per report, minutes and cost formatting, CSV writing | `internal/logread` |\n\nParsing the log, aggregating, and formatting are separate functions, each unit-tested. Business\nlogic lives in `internal/logread` and `internal/report`; `internal/cli` only wires them together.\n\n## Shared Types and Interfaces\n\n```go\n// internal/logread\ntype Record struct {\n    ExecutionID  string\n    StartedAt    string // RFC 3339 UTC; the first 10 characters are the start date\n    Status       string\n    Command      string // job.command_name\n    Target       string // job.target\n    Provider     string // job.llm\n    Model        string // result.stats.model when non-empty, otherwise job.model\n    InputTokens  int64\n    CachedTokens int64\n    OutputTokens int64\n    CostUSD      float64\n    ElapsedMS    int64\n    ReturnCode   *int // nil when null or absent\n    TimedOut     bool\n}\n\nfunc (r Record) Date() string   // StartedAt[:10]\nfunc (r Record) Failed() bool   // Status != \"succeeded\"\n\ntype Result struct {\n    Records []Record // kept records in file order\n    Skipped int      // skipped lines in the whole file\n}\n\nfunc Read(r io.Reader, warn io.Writer) (Result, error)\nfunc ReadFile(path string, warn io.Writer) (Result, error)\n```\n\n```go\n// internal/cli\ntype Options struct {\n    Report string\n    Target string\n    HasTarget bool\n    Since  string // YYYY-MM-DD or empty\n    Until  string // YYYY-MM-DD or empty\n    Log    string\n}\n\nfunc Parse(args []string) (Options, error)\nfunc Run(args []string, stdout, stderr io.Writer) int\n```\n\n`Run` uses a local `flag.FlagSet` with `flag.ContinueOnError` or an equivalent hand parser, never\nthe global flag set, and is tested with `bytes.Buffer`. Report output is buffered and written to\nstandard output only after the log has been read successfully, so a failing command leaves\nstandard output empty.\n\n## Execution Flow\n\nparse arguments, open the log, ingest records applying the skip rules, apply the Target and date\nfilters, compute one report, write CSV. The `skipped` count is computed over the whole file before\nany filter applies.\n\n## Shared Output Rules\n\nCSV with one header row and `\\n` line endings; minutes written as tenths with\n`t = (ms + 3000) / 6000` in integer division and formatted `t/10`, `.`, `t%10`; cost written with\n`%.4f`; text ordered by byte value; a `TOTAL` row of zeros when no record matches, where the report\nhas one.\n\n## Exit Codes\n\n| Code | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| 0 | Success, including skipped lines or no matching records |\n| 1 | The log cannot be opened or read |\n| 2 | Usage error |\n\nDiagnostics go to standard error and are never compared.\n\n## Persistence Ownership\n\n| Boundary | Owner module |\n|---|---|\n| Persistence, configuration, file stores, external services | None; the application has no database and no external service. The log is read-only input. |\n\n## Technical Decisions\n\n- Records that fail any rule never register their `execution_id`, so a later valid record with that\n  id is kept.\n- `--help` is an unknown option and exits 2 with the usage line on standard error.\n- A repeated option keeps its last occurrence.\n- A directory given as the log path is an unreadable log and exits 1.\n- A numeric field with a JSON type other than number or `null` counts as zero.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC foundation-builds ===\nIntent: The module builds the ddkstat executable from ./cmd/ddkstat with the Go toolchain.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport os\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\nimport tempfile\n\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as workdir:\n    binary = os.path.join(workdir, \"ddkstat\")\n    result = subprocess.run(\n        [\"go\", \"build\", \"-o\", binary, \"./cmd/ddkstat\"],\n        capture_output=True,\n        text=True,\n    )\n    print(result.stdout)\n    print(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\n    assert result.returncode == 0\n    assert os.path.isfile(binary)\n=== END AC foundation-builds ===\n\n=== AC foundation-vet ===\nIntent: go vet passes across the whole module.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; no third-party module and no database.\n- Never modify, rewrite, or substitute any file under `sources/`.\n- Application code never reads `sources/run.sh` and never embeds a report row, count, or total.\n- Never write to the log being read.\n- Only `cmd/ddkstat` calls `os.Exit`.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-CommandLine.md ===\n# FEATURE: Command Line\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261026 V1 |\n| Description | Parse the ddkstat command line into a report name, the three options, and exactly one trailing log path. |\n| Depends On  | ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides    | ddkstat <report> [--target <name>] [--since <YYYY-MM-DD>] [--until <YYYY-MM-DD>] <log> |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nThe command is `ddkstat <report> [--target <name>] [--since <date>] [--until <date>] <log>`. The\nreport comes first, options follow it, and the log path is last. This feature delivers the\nparser and the executable entry point at `./cmd/ddkstat`; date validation, usage-error exit codes,\nand unreadable-log handling belong to their own features.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `<report>` is one of `summary`, `commands`, `targets`, `models`, `daily`, `failures`.\n- The accepted options are `--target <name>`, `--since <date>`, and `--until <date>`, each taking\n  one value, in any order, between the report and the log path.\n- Exactly one log path is the final argument.\n- A repeated option keeps its last occurrence.\n- `cli.Parse(args []string) (Options, error)` returns the typed `Options` from `ARCHITECTURE.md`,\n  and returns a non-nil error for any argument list it cannot accept. `cli.Run` maps that error to\n  the usage-error exit code in the usage-errors feature.\n- `go build ./cmd/ddkstat` produces the executable using only the standard library.\n\n## Required Unit Tests\n\nThe build writes these tests in `internal/cli`, each against real parser output:\n\n| Test | Behavior |\n|---|---|\n| `TestParseAcceptsEveryReport` | Each of the six report names parses with a log path. |\n| `TestParseOptionsFollowReport` | The three options parse in any order and populate `Options`. |\n| `TestParseLogPathIsLast` | The final argument becomes `Options.Log`. |\n| `TestParseRepeatedOptionLastWins` | A repeated option keeps its last value. |\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC cli-parse-every-report ===\nIntent: Every one of the six report names with a log path is accepted as a valid command line, so none exits with the usage-error code.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport json\nimport os\nimport tempfile\n\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\nrecord = {\n    \"execution_id\": \"20261014.020414.582Z-db1fe4ea\",\n    \"started_at\": \"2026-10-14T02:04:14.582Z\",\n    \"status\": \"succeeded\",\n    \"job\": {\"command_name\": \"analyze\", \"target\": \"Toml\", \"llm\": \"gemini\", \"model\": \"gemini-3-flash\"},\n}\nusage_error = 2\nreports = [\"summary\", \"commands\", \"targets\", \"models\", \"daily\", \"failures\"]\n\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as workdir:\n    log = os.path.join(workdir, \"llm.jsonl\")\n    with open(log, \"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as handle:\n        handle.write(json.dumps(record) + \"\\n\")\n    for report in reports:\n        code, out, err = cli_runner(report, log)\n        print(report, code, err)\n        assert code != usage_error, (report, code, out, err)\n=== END AC cli-parse-every-report ===\n\n=== AC cli-parse-options ===\nIntent: The three options between the report and the log path, in either order, are accepted and do not exit with the usage-error code.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport json\nimport os\nimport tempfile\n\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\nrecord = {\n    \"execution_id\": \"20261015.061132.155Z-a5a1d0d4\",\n    \"started_at\": \"2026-10-15T06:11:32.155Z\",\n    \"status\": \"succeeded\",\n    \"job\": {\"command_name\": \"analyze\", \"target\": \"CommonMark\", \"llm\": \"claude\", \"model\": \"sonnet\"},\n}\nusage_error = 2\n\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as workdir:\n    log = os.path.join(workdir, \"llm.jsonl\")\n    with open(log, \"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as handle:\n        handle.write(json.dumps(record) + \"\\n\")\n    orders = [\n        [\"--target\", \"CommonMark\", \"--since\", \"2026-10-01\", \"--until\", \"2026-10-31\"],\n        [\"--until\", \"2026-10-31\", \"--since\", \"2026-10-01\", \"--target\", \"CommonMark\"],\n        [\"--since\", \"2026-10-01\"],\n        [\"--until\", \"2026-10-31\"],\n        [\"--target\", \"CommonMark\"],\n    ]\n    for options in orders:\n        code, out, err = cli_runner(\"summary\", *options, log)\n        print(options, code, err)\n        assert code != usage_error, (options, code, out, err)\n=== END AC cli-parse-options ===\n\n=== AC cli-parse-unit-tests ===\nIntent: The parser unit tests required by this feature exist and pass.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nnames = [\n    \"TestParseAcceptsEveryReport\",\n    \"TestParseOptionsFollowReport\",\n    \"TestParseLogPathIsLast\",\n    \"TestParseRepeatedOptionLastWins\",\n]\nfor name in names:\n    result = run_test(\"./internal/cli\", name)\n    print(name, result.status)\n    print(result.stdout)\n    assert result.passed, (name, result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC cli-parse-unit-tests ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- The report name is positional and comes first; the log path is positional and comes last.\n- `--help` is not special: it is an unknown option.\n- The parser uses a local flag set or hand parser with `ContinueOnError` semantics and never the global flag set; only `cmd/ddkstat` calls `os.Exit`.\n- Standard library only.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-LogReading.md ===\n# FEATURE: Log Reading\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261026 V1 |\n| Description | Read the Drydock JSON Lines log line by line, ignoring blank lines and numbering every line. |\n| Depends On  | ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides    | logread.Read, logread.ReadFile |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nThe log is JSON Lines in UTF-8, one JSON object per line. This feature delivers the line reader in\n`internal/logread`: it walks the input, discards blank lines, and hands each non-blank line, with\nits 1-based line number, to the record decoder that the skip-rule features extend.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `logread.Read(r io.Reader, warn io.Writer) (logread.Result, error)` reads every line of `r`.\n- `logread.ReadFile(path string, warn io.Writer) (logread.Result, error)` opens the file, reads it\n  with `Read`, and closes it. It returns a non-nil error when the file cannot be opened or read,\n  including when the path names a directory.\n- A blank line, meaning empty or whitespace only, is ignored and is neither a record nor counted as\n  skipped.\n- Line numbers are 1-based and count blank lines, so a warning names the physical line.\n- Lines longer than the default `bufio.Scanner` token limit are read in full.\n- The log file is opened read-only and never written.\n- Each non-blank line is decoded as one JSON object into a `logread.Record`.\n\n## Required Unit Tests\n\nThe build writes these tests in `internal/logread`:\n\n| Test | Behavior |\n|---|---|\n| `TestReadIgnoresBlankLines` | Blank lines yield no record and no skipped count. |\n| `TestReadNumbersEveryLine` | The line number used for a warning counts preceding blank lines. |\n| `TestReadAcceptsLongLine` | A valid record line longer than 1 MiB is read as one record. |\n| `TestReadFileMissing` | `ReadFile` on a nonexistent path returns an error. |\n| `TestReadFileDirectory` | `ReadFile` on a directory returns an error. |\n| `TestReadNeverWritesLog` | After `ReadFile`, the log file bytes and modification time are unchanged. |\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC log-read-blank-lines ===\nIntent: Blank lines are ignored and not counted as records or skipped lines.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nfor name in (\"TestReadIgnoresBlankLines\", \"TestReadNumbersEveryLine\"):\n    result = run_test(\"./internal/logread\", name)\n    print(name, result.status)\n    print(result.stdout)\n    assert result.passed, (name, result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC log-read-blank-lines ===\n\n=== AC log-read-long-line ===\nIntent: A record line longer than the default scanner token limit is read in full.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nname = \"TestReadAcceptsLongLine\"\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/logread\", name)\nprint(name, result.status)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.passed, (name, result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC log-read-long-line ===\n\n=== AC log-read-file-errors ===\nIntent: ReadFile reports an error for a missing path and for a directory.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nfor name in (\"TestReadFileMissing\", \"TestReadFileDirectory\"):\n    result = run_test(\"./internal/logread\", name)\n    print(name, result.status)\n    print(result.stdout)\n    assert result.passed, (name, result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC log-read-file-errors ===\n\n=== AC log-read-read-only ===\nIntent: Reading a log leaves the log file unchanged.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; This feature adds the record validation rules to the\nreader: a line that fails any rule is skipped, counted in `Result.Skipped`, and reported with one\nwarning on the warning writer naming its line number. Skipping never changes the exit code.\n\n## Behavior\n\nA non-blank line is skipped when any of these holds:\n\n- It is not a JSON object, including a truncated line, a JSON array, a bare scalar, and `null`.\n- It is not valid UTF-8; it is treated as not a JSON object.\n- Any of `execution_id`, `started_at`, `status`, `job.command_name`, or `job.target` is absent or\n  not a non-empty string.\n- `started_at` does not begin with a real calendar date written `YYYY-MM-DD`, for example\n  `2026-13-01` or `2026-02-30`, because every report keys on the start date.\n\nEach skipped line writes exactly one warning to the warning writer that contains its 1-based line\nnumber. A skipped line never registers its `execution_id`, so a later valid record with that id is\nkept (see `FEATURE-DuplicateIds.md`). The skipped count covers the whole file and is independent of\nany filter applied later.\n\n## Required Unit Tests\n\nThe build writes these tests in `internal/logread`:\n\n| Test | Behavior |\n|---|---|\n| `TestSkipNotJSONObject` | Truncated JSON, an array, a scalar, and `null` are each skipped. |\n| `TestSkipInvalidUTF8` | A line with invalid UTF-8 bytes is skipped. |\n| `TestSkipMissingRequiredField` | For each of the five required fields, absent, empty, and non-string values skip the line. |\n| `TestSkipBadStartedAt` | A `started_at` without a real leading `YYYY-MM-DD` date skips the line. |\n| `TestSkipWarnsWithLineNumber` | One warning per skipped line names its physical line number, counting blank lines. |\n| `TestSkipDoesNotAffectValidRecords` | Valid lines around skipped lines are all kept, in file order. |\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC malformed-not-object ===\nIntent: Lines that are not JSON objects are skipped and counted rather than failing the read.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nname = \"TestSkipNotJSONObject\"\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/logread\", name)\nprint(name, result.status)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.passed, (name, result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC malformed-not-object ===\n\n=== AC malformed-invalid-utf8 ===\nIntent: A line that is not valid UTF-8 is skipped as not a JSON object.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nname = \"TestSkipInvalidUTF8\"\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/logread\", name)\nprint(name, result.status)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.passed, (name, result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC malformed-invalid-utf8 ===\n\n=== AC malformed-required-fields ===\nIntent: A line lacking a non-empty string in any required field is skipped.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nname = \"TestSkipBadStartedAt\"\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/logread\", name)\nprint(name, result.status)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.passed, (name, result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC malformed-started-at ===\n\n=== AC malformed-warning-and-count ===\nIntent: Each skipped line is warned about once with its line number and valid neighbours are kept.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; This feature makes the reader keep the first\noccurrence of each `execution_id` and skip every later repeat, counting it in `Result.Skipped` and\nwarning on the warning writer with its line number, without failing the run.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- A line is skipped as a duplicate when its `execution_id` equals that of an earlier kept record.\n- The first occurrence is kept, and later repeats are skipped even when their other fields differ.\n- Only a record that passes every other rule registers its `execution_id`. A line skipped as\n  malformed does not register its id, so a later valid record with the same id is kept.\n- Each duplicate writes one warning naming its 1-based line number.\n- Duplicate detection is exact string equality on `execution_id`.\n- The skipped count includes duplicates and malformed lines together, over the whole file.\n\n## Required Unit Tests\n\nThe build writes these tests in `internal/logread`:\n\n| Test | Behavior |\n|---|---|\n| `TestDuplicateFirstOccurrenceKept` | Two lines with one id yield one record, the first, and one skipped. |\n| `TestDuplicateKeepsFirstEvenWhenFieldsDiffer` | The kept record carries the first line's field values. |\n| `TestSkippedLineDoesNotRegisterID` | A malformed line followed by a valid line with the same id keeps the valid line. |\n| `TestDuplicateWarnsWithLineNumber` | Each duplicate produces one warning naming its physical line number. |\n| `TestDuplicateCountedWithMalformed` | Duplicates and malformed lines are both counted in `Skipped`. |\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC duplicate-first-kept ===\nIntent: The first occurrence of an execution_id is kept and later repeats are skipped and counted.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nfor name in (\"TestDuplicateFirstOccurrenceKept\", \"TestDuplicateKeepsFirstEvenWhenFieldsDiffer\"):\n    result = run_test(\"./internal/logread\", name)\n    print(name, result.status)\n    print(result.stdout)\n    assert result.passed, (name, result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC duplicate-first-kept ===\n\n=== AC duplicate-skipped-line-does-not-register ===\nIntent: A line skipped for another reason does not register its id, so a later valid record with that id is kept.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nname = \"TestSkippedLineDoesNotRegisterID\"\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/logread\", name)\nprint(name, result.status)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.passed, (name, result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC duplicate-skipped-line-does-not-register ===\n\n=== AC duplicate-warning-and-count ===\nIntent: Each duplicate warns once with its line number and is counted together with malformed lines.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; BEGIN ARTIFACT ARCHITECTURE.md ===\n# ARCHITECTURE: DrydockStats\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261026 V1 |\n| Description | Module layout, package boundaries, shared types, and technical decisions for the ddkstat LLM log reporter. |\n| Depends On  | None |\n| Provides    | go.mod, cmd/ddkstat, internal/cli, internal/logread, internal/report |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`ddkstat` is a command-line reporter for Drydock's LLM execution log (`logs/llm.jsonl`). It reads a\nJSON Lines file, skips malformed and duplicate lines, narrows the records by optional Target and\ndate filters, and writes one of six CSV reports to standard output. It has no database and no\nnetwork access, and it never writes to the log it reads.\n\n## Technology Stack\n\nDerived from `TECHNOLOGY_STACK.md`, which remains the decision of record.\n\n| Technology | Where it applies |\n|---|---|\n| Go 1.22 or newer, standard library only | All application code; no third-party modules |\n| `go test` and `go vet` | The test runner behind `bin/test.sh` |\n| JSON Lines | The input format, decoded with `encoding/json` |\n| POSIX sh | `bin/test.sh` and the supplied grader `sources/run.sh` |\n\n## Module Layout\n\n```text\ngo.mod                    module declaration, Go 1.22 or newer, no require lines\ncmd/ddkstat/main.go       package main; the only caller of os.Exit\ninternal/cli/             Run, argument parsing, exit codes, usage line\ninternal/logread/         reading, skip rules, de-duplication, record model\ninternal/report/          filters, aggregation, and CSV formatting as separate functions\nbin/test.sh               POSIX launcher for go vet and go test\n```\n\n## Package Boundaries\n\n| Package | Owns | May import |\n|---|---|---|\n| `cmd/ddkstat` | Process start; passes `os.Args[1:]`, `os.Stdout`, `os.Stderr` to `cli.Run`; exits with its result | `internal/cli` |\n| `internal/cli` | Argument parsing, date validation, exit codes, orchestration of read, filter, aggregate, format | `internal/logread`, `internal/report` |\n| `internal/logread` | Opening and reading the log, line numbering, skip and duplicate rules, the `Record` model | standard library |\n| `internal/report` | Filtering, aggregation per report, minutes and cost formatting, CSV writing | `internal/logread` |\n\nParsing the log, aggregating, and formatting are separate functions, each unit-tested. Business\nlogic lives in `internal/logread` and `internal/report`; `internal/cli` only wires them together.\n\n## Shared Types and Interfaces\n\n```go\n// internal/logread\ntype Record struct {\n    ExecutionID  string\n    StartedAt    string // RFC 3339 UTC; the first 10 characters are the start date\n    Status       string\n    Command      string // job.command_name\n    Target       string // job.target\n    Provider     string // job.llm\n    Model        string // result.stats.model when non-empty, otherwise job.model\n    InputTokens  int64\n    CachedTokens int64\n    OutputTokens int64\n    CostUSD      float64\n    ElapsedMS    int64\n    ReturnCode   *int // nil when null or absent\n    TimedOut     bool\n}\n\nfunc (r Record) Date() string   // StartedAt[:10]\nfunc (r Record) Failed() bool   // Status != \"succeeded\"\n\ntype Result struct {\n    Records []Record // kept records in file order\n    Skipped int      // skipped lines in the whole file\n}\n\nfunc Read(r io.Reader, warn io.Writer) (Result, error)\nfunc ReadFile(path string, warn io.Writer) (Result, error)\n```\n\n```go\n// internal/cli\ntype Options struct {\n    Report string\n    Target string\n    HasTarget bool\n    Since  string // YYYY-MM-DD or empty\n    Until  string // YYYY-MM-DD or empty\n    Log    string\n}\n\nfunc Parse(args []string) (Options, error)\nfunc Run(args []string, stdout, stderr io.Writer) int\n```\n\n`Run` uses a local `flag.FlagSet` with `flag.ContinueOnError` or an equivalent hand parser, never\nthe global flag set, and is tested with `bytes.Buffer`. Report output is buffered and written to\nstandard output only after the log has been read successfully, so a failing command leaves\nstandard output empty.\n\n## Execution Flow\n\nparse arguments, open the log, ingest records applying the skip rules, apply the Target and date\nfilters, compute one report, write CSV. The `skipped` count is computed over the whole file before\nany filter applies.\n\n## Shared Output Rules\n\nCSV with one header row and `\\n` line endings; minutes written as tenths with\n`t = (ms + 3000) / 6000` in integer division and formatted `t/10`, `.`, `t%10`; cost written with\n`%.4f`; text ordered by byte value; a `TOTAL` row of zeros when no record matches, where the report\nhas one.\n\n## Exit Codes\n\n| Code | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| 0 | Success, including skipped lines or no matching records |\n| 1 | The log cannot be opened or read |\n| 2 | Usage error |\n\nDiagnostics go to standard error and are never compared.\n\n## Persistence Ownership\n\n| Boundary | Owner module |\n|---|---|\n| Persistence, configuration, file stores, external services | None; the application has no database and no external service. The log is read-only input. |\n\n## Technical Decisions\n\n- Records that fail any rule never register their `execution_id`, so a later valid record with that\n  id is kept.\n- `--help` is an unknown option and exits 2 with the usage line on standard error.\n- A repeated option keeps its last occurrence.\n- A directory given as the log path is an unreadable log and exits 1.\n- A numeric field with a JSON type other than number or `null` counts as zero.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC foundation-builds ===\nIntent: The module builds the ddkstat executable from ./cmd/ddkstat with the Go toolchain.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport os\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\nimport tempfile\n\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as workdir:\n    binary = os.path.join(workdir, \"ddkstat\")\n    result = subprocess.run(\n        [\"go\", \"build\", \"-o\", binary, \"./cmd/ddkstat\"],\n        capture_output=True,\n        text=True,\n    )\n    print(result.stdout)\n    print(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\n    assert result.returncode == 0\n    assert os.path.isfile(binary)\n=== END AC foundation-builds ===\n\n=== AC foundation-vet ===\nIntent: go vet passes across the whole module.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\n\nresult = subprocess.run([\"go\", \"vet\", \"./...\"], capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\nassert result.returncode == 0\n=== END AC foundation-vet ===\n\n=== AC foundation-module-stdlib-only ===\nIntent: go.mod declares Go 1.22 or newer and requires no third-party module.\nSuite: scoped\n\nimport re\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\ntext = Path(\"go.mod\").read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\nmatch = re.search(r\"^go (\\d+)\\.(\\d+)\", text, re.M)\nassert match is not None\nassert (int(match.group(1)), int(match.group(2))) >= (1, 22)\nfor line in text.splitlines():\n    stripped = line.strip()\n    assert not stripped.startswith(\"require\"), stripped\n    assert not stripped.startswith(\"replace\"), stripped\n=== END AC foundation-module-stdlib-only ===\n\n=== AC foundation-layout ===\nIntent: The main package and the three internal packages exist, and only main calls os.Exit.\nSuite: scoped\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nassert Path(\"cmd/ddkstat/main.go\").is_file()\nfor package in (\"cli\", \"logread\", \"report\"):\n    directory = Path(\"internal\") / package\n    assert directory.is_dir(), package\n    assert list(directory.glob(\"*.go\")), package\n\nfor source in Path(\"internal\").rglob(\"*.go\"):\n    assert \"os.Exit(\" not in source.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"), str(source)\n=== END AC foundation-layout ===\n\n=== AC foundation-grader-independence ===\nIntent: Application code never reads sources/run.sh.\nSuite: scoped\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfor root in (\"cmd\", \"internal\"):\n    for source in Path(root).rglob(\"*.go\"):\n        if source.name.endswith(\"_test.go\"):\n            continue\n        assert \"run.sh\" not in source.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"), str(source)\n=== END AC foundation-grader-independence ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Standard library only; no third-party module and no database.\n- Never modify, rewrite, or substitute any file under `sources/`.\n- Application code never reads `sources/run.sh` and never embeds a report row, count, or total.\n- Never write to the log being read.\n- Only `cmd/ddkstat` calls `os.Exit`.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-CommandLine.md ===\n# FEATURE: Command Line\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261026 V1 |\n| Description | Parse the ddkstat command line into a report name, the three options, and exactly one trailing log path. |\n| Depends On  | ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides    | ddkstat <report> [--target <name>] [--since <YYYY-MM-DD>] [--until <YYYY-MM-DD>] <log> |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nThe command is `ddkstat <report> [--target <name>] [--since <date>] [--until <date>] <log>`. The\nreport comes first, options follow it, and the log path is last. This feature delivers the\nparser and the executable entry point at `./cmd/ddkstat`; date validation, usage-error exit codes,\nand unreadable-log handling belong to their own features.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `<report>` is one of `summary`, `commands`, `targets`, `models`, `daily`, `failures`.\n- The accepted options are `--target <name>`, `--since <date>`, and `--until <date>`, each taking\n  one value, in any order, between the report and the log path.\n- Exactly one log path is the final argument.\n- A repeated option keeps its last occurrence.\n- `cli.Parse(args []string) (Options, error)` returns the typed `Options` from `ARCHITECTURE.md`,\n  and returns a non-nil error for any argument list it cannot accept. `cli.Run` maps that error to\n  the usage-error exit code in the usage-errors feature.\n- `go build ./cmd/ddkstat` produces the executable using only the standard library.\n\n## Required Unit Tests\n\nThe build writes these tests in `internal/cli`, each against real parser output:\n\n| Test | Behavior |\n|---|---|\n| `TestParseAcceptsEveryReport` | Each of the six report names parses with a log path. |\n| `TestParseOptionsFollowReport` | The three options parse in any order and populate `Options`. |\n| `TestParseLogPathIsLast` | The final argument becomes `Options.Log`. |\n| `TestParseRepeatedOptionLastWins` | A repeated option keeps its last value. |\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC cli-parse-every-report ===\nIntent: Every one of the six report names with a log path is accepted as a valid command line, so none exits with the usage-error code.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport json\nimport os\nimport tempfile\n\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\nrecord = {\n    \"execution_id\": \"20261014.020414.582Z-db1fe4ea\",\n    \"started_at\": \"2026-10-14T02:04:14.582Z\",\n    \"status\": \"succeeded\",\n    \"job\": {\"command_name\": \"analyze\", \"target\": \"Toml\", \"llm\": \"gemini\", \"model\": \"gemini-3-flash\"},\n}\nusage_error = 2\nreports = [\"summary\", \"commands\", \"targets\", \"models\", \"daily\", \"failures\"]\n\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as workdir:\n    log = os.path.join(workdir, \"llm.jsonl\")\n    with open(log, \"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as handle:\n        handle.write(json.dumps(record) + \"\\n\")\n    for report in reports:\n        code, out, err = cli_runner(report, log)\n        print(report, code, err)\n        assert code != usage_error, (report, code, out, err)\n=== END AC cli-parse-every-report ===\n\n=== AC cli-parse-options ===\nIntent: The three options between the report and the log path, in either order, are accepted and do not exit with the usage-error code.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport json\nimport os\nimport tempfile\n\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\nrecord = {\n    \"execution_id\": \"20261015.061132.155Z-a5a1d0d4\",\n    \"started_at\": \"2026-10-15T06:11:32.155Z\",\n    \"status\": \"succeeded\",\n    \"job\": {\"command_name\": \"analyze\", \"target\": \"CommonMark\", \"llm\": \"claude\", \"model\": \"sonnet\"},\n}\nusage_error = 2\n\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as workdir:\n    log = os.path.join(workdir, \"llm.jsonl\")\n    with open(log, \"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as handle:\n        handle.write(json.dumps(record) + \"\\n\")\n    orders = [\n        [\"--target\", \"CommonMark\", \"--since\", \"2026-10-01\", \"--until\", \"2026-10-31\"],\n        [\"--until\", \"2026-10-31\", \"--since\", \"2026-10-01\", \"--target\", \"CommonMark\"],\n        [\"--since\", \"2026-10-01\"],\n        [\"--until\", \"2026-10-31\"],\n        [\"--target\", \"CommonMark\"],\n    ]\n    for options in orders:\n        code, out, err = cli_runner(\"summary\", *options, log)\n        print(options, code, err)\n        assert code != usage_error, (options, code, out, err)\n=== END AC cli-parse-options ===\n\n=== AC cli-parse-unit-tests ===\nIntent: The parser unit tests required by this feature exist and pass.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; the log path is positional and comes last.\n- `--help` is not special: it is an unknown option.\n- The parser uses a local flag set or hand parser with `ContinueOnError` semantics and never the global flag set; only `cmd/ddkstat` calls `os.Exit`.\n- Standard library only.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-LogReading.md ===\n# FEATURE: Log Reading\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261026 V1 |\n| Description | Read the Drydock JSON Lines log line by line, ignoring blank lines and numbering every line. |\n| Depends On  | ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides    | logread.Read, logread.ReadFile |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nThe log is JSON Lines in UTF-8, one JSON object per line. This feature delivers the line reader in\n`internal/logread`: it walks the input, discards blank lines, and hands each non-blank line, with\nits 1-based line number, to the record decoder that the skip-rule features extend.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `logread.Read(r io.Reader, warn io.Writer) (logread.Result, error)` reads every line of `r`.\n- `logread.ReadFile(path string, warn io.Writer) (logread.Result, error)` opens the file, reads it\n  with `Read`, and closes it. It returns a non-nil error when the file cannot be opened or read,\n  including when the path names a directory.\n- A blank line, meaning empty or whitespace only, is ignored and is neither a record nor counted as\n  skipped.\n- Line numbers are 1-based and count blank lines, so a warning names the physical line.\n- Lines longer than the default `bufio.Scanner` token limit are read in full.\n- The log file is opened read-only and never written.\n- Each non-blank line is decoded as one JSON object into a `logread.Record`.\n\n## Required Unit Tests\n\nThe build writes these tests in `internal/logread`:\n\n| Test | Behavior |\n|---|---|\n| `TestReadIgnoresBlankLines` | Blank lines yield no record and no skipped count. |\n| `TestReadNumbersEveryLine` | The line number used for a warning counts preceding blank lines. |\n| `TestReadAcceptsLongLine` | A valid record line longer than 1 MiB is read as one record. |\n| `TestReadFileMissing` | `ReadFile` on a nonexistent path returns an error. |\n| `TestReadFileDirectory` | `ReadFile` on a directory returns an error. |\n| `TestReadNeverWritesLog` | After `ReadFile`, the log file bytes and modification time are unchanged. |\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC log-read-blank-lines ===\nIntent: Blank lines are ignored and not counted as records or skipped lines.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nfor name in (\"TestReadIgnoresBlankLines\", \"TestReadNumbersEveryLine\"):\n    result = run_test(\"./internal/logread\", name)\n    print(name, result.status)\n    print(result.stdout)\n    assert result.passed, (name, result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC log-read-blank-lines ===\n\n=== AC log-read-long-line ===\nIntent: A record line longer than the default scanner token limit is read in full.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nname = \"TestReadAcceptsLongLine\"\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/logread\", name)\nprint(name, result.status)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.passed, (name, result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC log-read-long-line ===\n\n=== AC log-read-file-errors ===\nIntent: ReadFile reports an error for a missing path and for a directory.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nfor name in (\"TestReadFileMissing\", \"TestReadFileDirectory\"):\n    result = run_test(\"./internal/logread\", name)\n    print(name, result.status)\n    print(result.stdout)\n    assert result.passed, (name, result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC log-read-file-errors ===\n\n=== AC log-read-read-only ===\nIntent: Reading a log leaves the log file unchanged.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nname = \"TestReadNeverWritesLog\"\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/logread\", name)\nprint(name, result.status)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.passed, (name, result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC log-read-read-only ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- The log is opened read-only and is never written, truncated, or locked for writing.\n- A blank line is never counted as a record or as a skipped line.\n- Standard library only.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-MalformedLines.md ===\n# FEATURE: Malformed Lines\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261026 V1 |\n| Description | Skip lines that are not valid records, count them, and warn on standard error without failing the run. |\n| Depends On  | ARCHITECTURE.md, FEATURE-LogReading.md |\n| Provides    | logread.Result.Skipped |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nA malformed line must never fail the run. This feature adds the record validation rules to the\nreader: a line that fails any rule is skipped, counted in `Result.Skipped`, and reported with one\nwarning on the warning writer naming its line number. Skipping never changes the exit code.\n\n## Behavior\n\nA non-blank line is skipped when any of these holds:\n\n- It is not a JSON object, including a truncated line, a JSON array, a bare scalar, and `null`.\n- It is not valid UTF-8; it is treated as not a JSON object.\n- Any of `execution_id`, `started_at`, `status`, `job.command_name`, or `job.target` is absent or\n  not a non-empty string.\n- `started_at` does not begin with a real calendar date written `YYYY-MM-DD`, for example\n  `2026-13-01` or `2026-02-30`, because every report keys on the start date.\n\nEach skipped line writes exactly one warning to the warning writer that contains its 1-based line\nnumber. A skipped line never registers its `execution_id`, so a later valid record with that id is\nkept (see `FEATURE-DuplicateIds.md`). The skipped count covers the whole file and is independent of\nany filter applied later.\n\n## Required Unit Tests\n\nThe build writes these tests in `internal/logread`:\n\n| Test | Behavior |\n|---|---|\n| `TestSkipNotJSONObject` | Truncated JSON, an array, a scalar, and `null` are each skipped. |\n| `TestSkipInvalidUTF8` | A line with invalid UTF-8 bytes is skipped. |\n| `TestSkipMissingRequiredField` | For each of the five required fields, absent, empty, and non-string values skip the line. |\n| `TestSkipBadStartedAt` | A `started_at` without a real leading `YYYY-MM-DD` date skips the line. |\n| `TestSkipWarnsWithLineNumber` | One warning per skipped line names its physical line number, counting blank lines. |\n| `TestSkipDoesNotAffectValidRecords` | Valid lines around skipped lines are all kept, in file order. |\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC malformed-not-object ===\nIntent: Lines that are not JSON objects are skipped and counted rather than failing the read.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nname = \"TestSkipNotJSONObject\"\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/logread\", name)\nprint(name, result.status)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.passed, (name, result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC malformed-not-object ===\n\n=== AC malformed-invalid-utf8 ===\nIntent: A line that is not valid UTF-8 is skipped as not a JSON object.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nname = \"TestSkipInvalidUTF8\"\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/logread\", name)\nprint(name, result.status)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.passed, (name, result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC malformed-invalid-utf8 ===\n\n=== AC malformed-required-fields ===\nIntent: A line lacking a non-empty string in any required field is skipped.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nname = \"TestSkipMissingRequiredField\"\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/logread\", name)\nprint(name, result.status)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.passed, (name, result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC malformed-required-fields ===\n\n=== AC malformed-started-at ===\nIntent: A started_at that does not begin with a real calendar date skips the line.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nname = \"TestSkipBadStartedAt\"\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/logread\", name)\nprint(name, result.status)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.passed, (name, result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC malformed-started-at ===\n\n=== AC malformed-warning-and-count ===\nIntent: Each skipped line is warned about once with its line number and valid neighbours are kept.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nfor name in (\"TestSkipWarnsWithLineNumber\", \"TestSkipDoesNotAffectValidRecords\"):\n    result = run_test(\"./internal/logread\", name)\n    print(name, result.status)\n    print(result.stdout)\n    assert result.passed, (name, result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC malformed-warning-and-count ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Skipping never changes the exit code and never writes to standard output.\n- A skipped line never registers its `execution_id`.\n- Warnings go to standard error only and are never compared.\n- Standard library only.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-DuplicateIds.md ===\n# FEATURE: Duplicate Ids\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261026 V1 |\n| Description | Skip any line whose execution_id already appeared on an earlier kept record, keeping the first occurrence. |\n| Depends On  | ARCHITECTURE.md, FEATURE-LogReading.md, FEATURE-MalformedLines.md |\n| Provides    | logread.Dedupe |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nDrydock may write the same execution twice. This feature makes the reader keep the first\noccurrence of each `execution_id` and skip every later repeat, counting it in `Result.Skipped` and\nwarning on the warning writer with its line number, without failing the run.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- A line is skipped as a duplicate when its `execution_id` equals that of an earlier kept record.\n- The first occurrence is kept, and later repeats are skipped even when their other fields differ.\n- Only a record that passes every other rule registers its `execution_id`. A line skipped as\n  malformed does not register its id, so a later valid record with the same id is kept.\n- Each duplicate writes one warning naming its 1-based line number.\n- Duplicate detection is exact string equality on `execution_id`.\n- The skipped count includes duplicates and malformed lines together, over the whole file.\n\n## Required Unit Tests\n\nThe build writes these tests in `internal/logread`:\n\n| Test | Behavior |\n|---|---|\n| `TestDuplicateFirstOccurrenceKept` | Two lines with one id yield one record, the first, and one skipped. |\n| `TestDuplicateKeepsFirstEvenWhenFieldsDiffer` | The kept record carries the first line's field values. |\n| `TestSkippedLineDoesNotRegisterID` | A malformed line followed by a valid line with the same id keeps the valid line. |\n| `TestDuplicateWarnsWithLineNumber` | Each duplicate produces one warning naming its physical line number. |\n| `TestDuplicateCountedWithMalformed` | Duplicates and malformed lines are both counted in `Skipped`. |\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC duplicate-first-kept ===\nIntent: The first occurrence of an execution_id is kept and later repeats are skipped and counted.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nfor name in (\"TestDuplicateFirstOccurrenceKept\", \"TestDuplicateKeepsFirstEvenWhenFieldsDiffer\"):\n    result = run_test(\"./internal/logread\", name)\n    print(name, result.status)\n    print(result.stdout)\n    assert result.passed, (name, result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC duplicate-first-kept ===\n\n=== AC duplicate-skipped-line-does-not-register ===\nIntent: A line skipped for another reason does not register its id, so a later valid record with that id is kept.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nname = \"TestSkippedLineDoesNotRegisterID\"\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/logread\", name)\nprint(name, result.status)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.passed, (name, result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC duplicate-skipped-line-does-not-register ===\n\n=== AC duplicate-warning-and-count ===\nIntent: Each duplicate warns once with its line number and is counted together with malformed lines.\nSuite: scoped\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nfor name in (\"TestDuplicateWarnsWithLineNumber\", \"TestDuplicateCountedWithMalformed\"):\n    result = run_test(\"./internal/logread\", name)\n    print(name, result.status)\n    print(result.stdout)\n    assert result.passed, (name, result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC duplicate-warning-and-count ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- The first occurrence of an `execution_id` is always the one kept.\n- Skipping a duplicate never changes the exit code and never writes to standard output.\n- Standard library only.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===","ttft_ms":32118,"type":"result","duration_ms":86390,"uuid":"66245070-e7a5-4c8d-b29f-bd412cf25a86","ttft_stream_ms":2610,"time_to_request_ms":99,"first_content_frame_ms":2611,"queued_turn_count":0,"result_index":0,"model_usage_since_last_result":{"claude-sonnet-5-5":{"inputTokens":2,"outputTokens":14145,"cacheReadInputTokens":1568,"cacheCreationInputTokens":82717,"webSearchRequests":0,"costUSD":0.4724788,"contextWindow":1000000,"maxOutputTokens":128000,"thinkingTokens":3850,"canonicalModel":"claude-sonnet-5-5","provider":"firstParty","costBasis":"list"}}}
